Bountiful Nature Offers Prime Health

And it is this melody of the universal one-ness which should enthuse your common-sense and emotion to lov- Page 370

ing respect for the principles of nature. You, an entity in the great scheme, would prefer to join in the paean by consistency with nature in your manner of living and the use of your intelligence.

Beneficent, nature graciously offers the favors of the cosmic bounty for health and happiness. The only condition which she imposes is that of obeying the principles to which there is no exception in the universal one-ness. In my concept I simply interpret the altruism of nature to you and tender a few suggestions which I have taken from the cosmic code. Page 371

Food Suggestions as Garnered From Various 

Sources 

Perhaps these Food Suggestions may appeal as worth while to you and yours. You should, of course, obtain foods of quality and allocate them in healthful combinations. The quantities, also, must be determined by you.

Remember, however, that "we live by what we digest and not alone by what we eat." Therefore, a lessened quantity of the more natural diet should compensate for wastes better than a larger quantity of the less natural with its waste of digestion-functioning and organism-overtaxing.

No two kinds of food derived from any animal sources at one meal, excepting a minimum of butter as a spread and a minimum of cream as a flavor. Likewise, cereal products and nuts, which are so-called protein foods, should not be used with the animal-food proteins. That is, no two proteins at any one meal, but always one at each meal.

No fried foods of any kind; thus, cooked foods may be boiled, stewed, roasted, baked, grilled, etc.

No pork or animal food other than cooked, fresh, lean beef, mutton, poultry, fish; or cottage cheese, freshly made; or eggs, soft-boiled, coddled or poached, or preferably raw yolks of eggs.

No milk or cream which has been heated or otherwise preserved. 

No nuts of any kind other than freshly shredded cocoanuts, recently shelled and grated sweet almonds, or grated pecans.

Page 372

No hot bread of any kind other than very thin slices of crisp toast (slices dried 24 hours before toasting), or freshly oven-heated wafers, crackers, shredded wheat biscuits, etc.

No soups containing meat stocks, white flour, or other protein foods as above mentioned. 

Drink a quart of fresh, whole milk every 24 hours; however, none with other protein food meals. 

Use pure, strained honey, as may be desired. 

Use a minimum of sugar and salt. 

Use no candy or other artificial sweets, preserves, jams, etc.

Use no highly seasoned condiments, no stimulants of any kind, such as alcohol, charged water, certain drugs, coffee, tea, chocolate, cocoa, tobacco or concoctions of beverages made from hot water, hot milk, or other hot fluids.

Drink no water other than some cool (not iced) fresh water after finishing each meal. 

Use no potatoes of any kind, no Hubbard squash, no so-called starch foods. 

Use prunes, raisins (seedless), apricots, pears, apples, peaches and other sun-dried, edible fruits, either in the raw state, if desired, or soaked for 24 hours in cool, fresh water. Do not stew fruits in hot water. 

Use no vinegar at any time; but use freshly squeezed lemon juice freely. Use nothing preserved in vinegar. 

Use cold-pressed olive oil freely; if not cold-pressed, use sparingly.

Page 373

Use one kind of nuts at a meal without animal or cereal food to supply for fat-content deficiency. 

Use one raw vegetable or fruit, at least, with each meal and not as an after-course salad. The vitamin content is, in its natural state, of such health-giving and disease-preventing power as to compensate generally for the weakened power of vitamins which have been injured or impaired by some cooking temperatures. 

Use a teaspoonful of cod-liver oil every 24 hours, provided there is neither time nor opportunity for an hour or more exposure to the midday sunshine, or skyshine, or a proper ultraviolet sunlamp, with equipment, is unavailable.

Cook raw vegetables and fruits (canned, when raw is unobtainable) in as little water as possible, seasoned with a minimum of butter; no white flour sauces.

Leafy, raw vegetables, to be chopped up before serving. Hard vegetables, prepared just before serving, to be grated, shredded, etc., when practicable; otherwise, to be chopped up fine, to assist in thorough chewing and salivating and to lessen fermentation. 

Beets, parsnips, lettuce, onions, garlic and cabbage are gas-forming. 

Cooking vegetables and fruits: Celery, spinach, string beans, asparagus, egg plant, beet tops, turnip tops, turnips, carrots, pumpkin, oyster plant, kale, fruits, berries. 

Fresh, green vegetables and fruits: Celery, spinach, asparagus, parsley, carrots, turnips, endives, olives, fruits, berries, melons, nuts.

Page 374

Dried fruits (raw or prepared as above suggested): 

Prunes, figs, seedless raisins, apricots, pears, apples, peaches, and other sun-dried, edible fruits.

Note: The aforementioned, accustomed foods, stimulants, etc., which are proscribed, should be gradually lessened in use somewhat thus-nonuse not to be completed until the fifth week's modification by middleaged persons (28 to 49 years), the ninth and tenth weeks for elderly persons (49 to 70 years), and not for many months for old persons (beyond 70 years). (See modification suggestion on the next page.)

As the body is sustained by food, nothing need be said in order to demonstrate that nutritious food is necessary for preserving sound health. Everybody knows that we live by what we eat. Napoleon's saying that an army marches on its stomach is quoted as often in the domestic kitchen as in the barracks messroom, and every woman laughingly repeats that old adage, which no doubt dates from the dawn of civilization, that a man's heart is in his palate and his love and admiration for his partner are determined by the table which she prepares for him.

It is possible that tincan and carton dietetics is at least a contributing cause in the increase of divorce in recent years. But I do not consider this likely, as the average man has no idea what he eats, and does not seem to care as long as his food looks and tastes pleasing. The average woman can live on gossip and small quantities of confections, creams and aesthetic delicacies. As I have said, most of us eat with our eyes and palate without regard to quality, quantity or opportuneness.

Page 375

The Lady and the Tin Cans 

Woman can murder man or assist in prolonging his life almost indefinitely by her management of the food supply and its preparation and serving. She may fill him up with tasty things extracted at random from cans and boxes, artistically decorated, and collect his life insurance and seize his estate before she ages. Or she may rely on her usual diplomacy and provide him with nutritious food dressed up in imitation of the killing stuff which he prefers, and have him hanging around, if he is worth keeping and her nerves can stand his presence and coming and going, long after his hairs gray. 

The common sense which enabled me to develop this health concept and plan would restrain me from attempting to give advice on family relationship and household management. I say no more than that wholesome food, scientifically selected, allocated and served, would enhance happiness in the home. It would, I fear, be imprudent to say more than this. 

An impression seems to prevail that proper and adequate nutrition is obtained by quantity and savor of the food eaten and gorging whenever the eye or palate is hungry. Food is fuel, and it seems to make no difference whether it is smoky and gassy bituminous, or coke, or anthracite; just throw enough into the furnace-door and gulp down the lumps. To be healthy, the idea is, eat all that the stomach can hold and often enough to feel uncomfortable or leaden-headed.

Cartons Instead of Vegetables 

I have no objection to food in tin cans and cartons. 

However, fresh substance is, as a matter of course, preferable. When the fresh is not available, the canned Page 376

must be satisfactory. The choice and preparation of food are as important as the quality and quantity. In many instances, the selection and preparation are so deleterious to health that one cannot help fearing that the cook used the cans, cartons and other containers instead of the food.

I am almost afraid to state that the choice and preparation of food has become a science. This is so; but the average person dislikes the idea of being obliged to absorb vitamines and calories in mathematical proportions and to live under a discipline as to quantity and variety. He classes dining as a delectable indulgence, in which he must have the utmost freedom to pick what he likes and swallow by quantity or to satiety. He gets his fill of science in his office or store and in the newspapers and magazines, and he would bar it from the dining-room and kitchen. 

Diet suggests medical treatment, discipline and domestic science to the average person; and he lifts his eyes and hands in despair. He craves food, not heat units and energizers. He would rather have stomach ache or dyspepsia than submit to science at the gasrange. He would eat the most pernicious incompatibles in order to show his independence.

So, I am not discussing diet or dieting, but food, and not discipline, but latitude and selectivity. You may eat what you like, provided it is not obviously unhealthy, and as much as you want, provided you do not stuff.

Variety of Palatable Foods 

The rules concerning food are few. Food must be chosen with proper regard for its health value. In vari- Page 377

ety and quantity it must be proportioned to the known requirements of the body. Food should be plainly prepared. It should be masticated thoroughly. 

I do not present to you a diet routine. I confine my recommendations to the accompanying table, which shows some foods which are beneficial and some which are injurious, and which shows how the varieties should be determined and how the foods should be prepared and furnished. I indicate some which are natural foods, and some which are not.

Quantity of food is an important question. However, quantity is not correctly volume, but measure by sustenance efficiency. Variety does not contemplate a diversity to please the palate, but a scope of healthgiving values. Quantity and variety are judged according to values in terms of food properties for bodyupbuilding and upkeep. The foodstuffs chosen and prepared according to the rules can and should be pleasing in looks and taste. Eating properly for better health should not be a torture; on the contrary, it should be as much like feasting as to eat like voracious wild animals. 

The accompanying table indicates some foods which are good, and some which are not. It explains how the foods should be chosen and prepared. The details of dieting as a scientific problem I leave to you to work out in a way adapted to your environment and health needs, that is to formulate your own plan. Every woman is well informed as to calories and so on, and the quantities of units required, and is competent to build up a system of her own with this table and information as the guide.

Page 378

Limit to Quantity 

Proper food in proper varieties and quantities is essential to good health. It is, in fact, part of the foundation of good health. It must be eaten correctly, and the meals should be so divided that, as with the alternation of exercise and rest, there would be such equalizing of eating and resting as to facilitate digestion. You will notice I have, in connection with the schedule, arranged the periods of the meals in conformity with the alternating activity and quiescence of the upper and lower digestive tracts and with opportunity for evacuation of waste. 

The saying that we live by what we eat should be expanded to we live by what we eat and digest. First we eat the proper foods properly at the proper times, and next we absorb the nutrition naturally and efficiently by adjusting eating to the period of rest regulating eating and digestion to the natural functioning of the upper and lower digestive tracts and to the natural rhythm of the stomach.

I have explained elsewhere in much detail the functioning of the upper and lower digestive tracts and the natural rhythm of the stomach. I have indicated in the schedule how eating is timed with periods of bodily rest and digestion is timed with the respective periods of exercise. In eating and digesting, also, there is the alternation of exercise and rest. 

You Arrange Your Own Card 

You may arrange your food selection to suit your particular situation from the suggestions in the accompanying table. You see here what substances are desir- Page 379

able and how they may be prepared. The table is a general guide. You get from the schedule an idea as to how to arrange your eating times to the alternation of rest and exercise. I lay down the principles, and you adapt them to your circumstances in a plan of your own. 

It would be practically impossible to attach too great importance to foods. Life is maintained by them, and health sustained. The foods containing the materials which the body needs are the foods which we should eat. 

We should eat them in the variety and quantity indicated by the body needs. We should adjust eating to the body's passivity periods and digestion to the body's respective activity periods, and time the eating and digesting to the natural rhythm of the stomach. To a great extent the stomach and its fuel are determinatives of bad health or good.

A Modification Schedule Suggestion 

According to the general plan which I have recommended to you in the preceding pages, you would undertake to develop for yourself a system of your own for the alternation of rest and exercise, with the object of regulating the shift from exercise to rest, or vice versa, at or as near as possible the precise time or opportunity at which each would be required. 

I simply advise you to use your primitive science, the common or horse sense, in adopting a modification schedule which seems to you to be consistent with your individual peculiarities and the exigencies of your mode of work and living. I assume, of course, that there never can be a rule of this kind equally applicable to two or more persons, as no two or more persons can be standardized.

Page 380

In my concept I stress the opportune alternation of exercise and rest and proper nutrition in proper quantity, variety and quality. Opportune alternation of exercise and rest provides each when it is conditionally most effective for maintaining constant prime of health.

For the twenty-one years following middle age, that is, from 49 to 70 years, being the division comprising elderly persons, each week should probably be doubled; then for old persons, of 70 years or more, each week should be quadrupled or more, depending on the degree of infirmity. If it is desirable to revert to the beginning, a tapering off of one day for each week in the table of modification schedule should meet requirements; and proportionately for longer modifications. 

EXPLANATION OF THE SCHEDULE CHAPTER I. 

That every one must make due adjustment of his time and mode of living to the important duties of his employment must be conceded. That there are certain domestic obligations to which the individual must adjust his spare time and his habits must also be conceded. That every individual has idiosyncracies, physical and mental, likewise is unquestionable. I take these and other considerations into account in offering to you suggestions for carrying out my health-plan based on natural principles.

The schedule which I here present is a standard. I do not expect all to adopt it in its entirety, although I am confident that some will. You are invited to follow it as closely as your circumstances will allow; or, in any event, to use it as a model from which to construct a plan of your own, suited strictly to yourself and your environment, conforming to the fundamentals laid down in my concept. I do expect you to accept the fundamentals and apply them in your manner of living, for these are the foundation of the robust health Page 383

and constant prime by which you are assured of a long life and a life full of the happiness which goes with continuous good health.

Natural Requirements Fundamental 

The base of the plan is the alternation of exercise and rest at physical opportunity, and the use of the proper nourishment for sustaining health at prime. By physical opportunism in the alternation of exercise and rest I mean that exercise or rest, as either might be taken, should be regulated to the moment of requirement. Instead, for example, of taking all your rest in six to nine hours of sleep at night, you would take some as you would need it during the day, as it is then timely, or opportune, and consequently more effectual. 

The underlying principle is, as I have explained in detail elsewhere, that the constructive or reproductive process is stimulated, and the decompositional process prevented from being physically deteriorative, by taking rest or exercise at the opportunity, in natural functioning, when the need exists. I have elsewhere shown to you that opportune alternation of exercise and rest is far more beneficial than protracted exercise, and rest taken at long intervals, in which the body is fatigued and stagnated.

The foregoing interprets the principle which you are requested to apply in adjusting your own plan to the standard which this schedule represents. You should try to alternate exercise and rest as opportunely as possible. 

From the accompanying schedule you may figure out a more or less easy plan with which to begin applying Page 384

the principle, and thereafter develop the plan as near to the standard as your circumstances will allow. 

Short and Long Vacations 

It would be advisable for you, or anybody, to take a short vacation, say of one to five weeks, depending on the state of your health, and try out the standard schedule. You should go into the country, and leave your business and worries in town, and, while recreating, live up to these directions as closely as you can. 

Very few persons are in prime of health. Virtually all occasionally need a health-recuperating vacation. 

Following this schedule would restore you to the prime of the best health and you would be in the physical and mental condition to take up a plan of your own, elaborated from this schedule, to maintain constant prime of health. I dare say that you are not now in satisfactory physical condition, and, therefore, are not as efficient and energetic as you would like to be and should be. 

Every one should take a vacation of a day at least every week. He should get out of his regular environment. He should get away from his work and his home. 

This would not only be invigorating to health, but would also be stimulating to the mind. A person would obtain new ideas and broaden his vision by such a diversion. 

The average person should take the vacation which I suggest on adopting these recommendations, in order to recuperate satisfactorily. The average person needs a vacation of this kind. 

Most Persons Need to Recuperate

One whose health is run down, or feels that his health Page 385

is depreciating, should, by all means, take a vacation of one to five weeks and follow this schedule as closely as possible.

Most of us nowadays are at the verge of physical collapse, and such a vacation would probably be the means of obviating disaster. The assertions of leading physicians as to modern diseases are alarming enough to induce virtually everybody to take a vacation for recuperating and ascertain just how healthy he is or is not. 

To most persons the vacation which I advise is absolutely necessary. 

If you have fears as to health, do not fail to take the vacation. If you feel a little run down, take it. If you feel well, and can spare the time, take it. If you cannot get away, then take the one-day vacation. And arrange to take this one-day vacation regularly in the future.

Use a tapering method in developing your own plan for alternating exercise and rest and consuming proper nourishment. Look upon this schedule as the standard which you will ultimately adopt; but work up to it gradually. I have indicated in the table how this tapering should be done. However, you may arrange the tapering to suit yourself. If you will only accept the principle and give it a fair trial, you will find out for yourself that my concept offers you the natural scheme for preserving health at prime.

Modify Standard to Your Circumstances 

The sequence of the schedule is the same for all ages. 

Again, I remind you that the schedule is the standard, but you must work out your own plan. The specified Page 386

arrangement is to eat and immediately relax (rest) for one hour at least, to be followed immediately by sleep in each period while sleep is continued. Then play or work for the time outlined in each period; then alternating dry rubs and sponge or shower baths; then sunshine exposure or ultraviolet radiation on the back only for five minutes; then toilet.

Throughout this schedule compensate equal durations of alternating activities (exercise) and passivities (rest); thus, beginning with four hours of exercise, followed by four hours' rest, three times in twenty-four hours, and ultimating in one hour's exercise, followed by one hour's rest, twelve times in twenty-four hoursall to attain to functioning regularity consistent with the stomach's rhythm of one hour's activity and one hour's passivity. The one-hour occupation of the stomach approximates the one-hour evacuation of the lower digestion tract's "completed" wastes, and vice versa; thus, diet for stomach and drainage for the lower tract hourly alternate with the diet for the lower tract and drainage for the stomach.

I have submitted a standard covering proper nourishment, with various foods and their preparation. You should regard food as medicine, and medicine as food. 

By using the proper nourishment and taking care of your organism according to these suggestions, you do away with the need of medicine, or, at any rate, of much medicine, as you prevent disease with it. Medicine is no more than concentrated food or foods prescribed to meet certain physical deficiencies. Air and sunlight are other foods which the body requires, and these are included in the schedule. Page 387

Sunshine and Fresh Air 

Airbaths and sunbaths are provided for. Even on cloudy days the sunbath should be taken, as skyshine is beneficial. Skyshine has been measured, and has been found to have about one-half the value of sunshine. 

The natural modification which is proposed is several days of skyshine, shaded from the sunshine; then, next day the feet are exposed to the sunshine three times for five minutes each time, and the next day for ten-minute periods, with the knees also exposed, and the next day for fifteen-minute periods, including the waist, and the next day for twenty-minute periods for the whole body; thereafter, five minutes more each day graduated up to one hour of sunshine or skyshine at exercise to one hour at rest.

Practically full-value rays may be had at any time in any place where a proper electrical supply is obtainable by using a mercury-vapor sunlamp, such as that made by the General Electric Company. This lamp "filters out" practically all the long and short ultraviolet and infra-red rays which are filtered out from sunlight by the atmosphere.

Medium-length ultraviolet and infra-red rays on a clean skin exposure are powerful factors in promoting organism welfare. Such purified air for lung and skin secretions not only induces the removal of blood impurities or wastes, but also by the same means provides ample oxygen compounds for the blood supply. With such natural opportunity for benefiting the bloodstream, there is afforded an equally natural opportunity for benefiting the sympathetic nervous system, as the Page 388

infra-red rays of heat exert an activity on the superficial nerve endings of the skin, which in turn influence activity on all nerves of the organism. 

Alternation for Health Vigor 

Such exposure at the organism's exercise arouses the organism to use the united yields of life-energy from digestion and respiration for proper functioning; while, on the other hand, unexposure at rest soothes or calms activity to promote rest for rebuilding depleted cellstructures; so that the organism uses the upper-digestion tract cell-structure's assimilation-supply to rebuild without undue energy-yield disturbance; that is, restful rest! ! !

Sunlight is one of the most necessary of foods. It must be classed as a food, as, although it is neither a solid nor a liquid, it is, nevertheless, a requisite for bodily sustenance. The chemical effects of sunlight we do get indirectly through the action of the sun in fruits and vegetables, and, in fact, all things that we eat, including animal foods. But these benefits of the sunbeam are only partially sufficient. We must eat the sun, as it were, by absorbing the energy and heat directly through clean and permeable skin. 

Dirt is supposed to be healthy. Perhaps it is salubrious as a food; those who eat it are better qualified to give you an opinion. Boys in particular are believed to be healthier and happier when they are coated thickly with dirt in their play, and it is thought to be good for children to roll in the earth and get as dirty as the dogs that romp with them. I admit frankly that I like to Page 389

see children, especially boys, get dirty at play; I rather prefer to see them looking more like humans than angels; dirt gathered all over their faces, hands and clothes in play seems paradoxically to give children character. However, the sunlight does not penetrate dirt, clothes or any coating, and the skin positively must be clean for the sunbath. I do not recommend dirt for the health of grownups. 

Water Is as Popular as Jazz

I am compelled to make one honest and somewhat flattering admission concerning the existing, foolish generation. This is that it is addicted to water baths. 

Though it may consider godliness next to bodily cleanliness, instead of the reverse, and apparently is not fastidious as to filth in books, periodicals, pictures, plays and conversation, it is far advanced in the use of water on the outside of the body. The generation has made great progress in personal cleanliness, and I question whether it could be grateful enough to the soap manufacturers for their clever advertising.

The appearance or simulation of culture and wealth probably has had much to do with interest in this subject. Most of the young persons of this age wear silk or rayon underwear and hosiery, and they would not feel exactly right in putting this clothing on over dirt; moreover, it would not seem quite appropriate and would be uneconomical to soil the clothing. So, we are obliged to admit that external refinement or the simulation of culture and wealth is doing some good in the health line.

Page 390

I would not say that the enthusiasm for bathing is a throwback to the reputed origin of man. It has not been proved that man in an embryonic stage was a fish. 

Neither do I believe that cleanliness was taken up for improvement of health. I think that it resulted chiefly from wearing fine clothes and "putting on a front."

In all probability, we have to thank the style designers and the manufacturers and merchants of soap and women's and men's wear for the popularity of the bath. 

We should not overlook the enterprise of municipalities and water companies in making clean, purified water as cheap and convenient as dirt.

Sun Jilts Face-Lotion Beauties 

In regard to health, and without considering art or aesthetics, or, if you will, vanity, science apprises us that rouge, powder, cosmetics, and so on, are not helps. 

The sun will not penetrate these mineral and chemical adornments on ladies' faces any more than it will the other kind of earth on boys' faces.

But it is superfluous for me to tell you that cleanliness is more important than godliness. You have learned this well from the modistes, clothing makers and dealers and soap purveyors. It only remains for me to advise you to remove rouge, cosmetics, grease or any other coating from your skin when taking a sunbath, and to urge insistently to you that the sunlight is more necessary to your health than water.

I might, perchance, have more success in convincing you as to the beneficence of the sun if I emphasized the beautifying over the health-preserving efficacy of the Page 391

ultraviolet and infrared rays. Beauty is more than skindeep, and I can conscientiously state that the sunlight does give smoothness and finish to the skin and does make the complexion velvety. While the sunlight, permeating the pores, invigorates and energizes the cellstructure and builds up health, it simultaneously enhances the condition of the texture and color of the skin. No complexion is so beautiful as that which the reasonable use of the sunlight develops. 

Not Necessarily on the Farm

Sunlight I specify as a food, indispensable and of the greatest value. I could not even consider omitting it from the standard-plan for better health. You must take quantities of it as you do take other kinds of food. 

Not the least among the advantages of the vacation which I recommend would be that of taking measures of sunlight in rural environment. There the sunlight comes down through pure, fresh air in its most beneficial natural state, and it would probably be more effective than anything else in curing whatever minor ailment you might have and recuperating you to perfect health.

I have quoted prominent physicians to prove that sunlight is one of the best medicines. Many diseases which resist other treatment are cured by sunlight. 

Several large sanitariums which specialize in sunlight hygiene are in operation, and they report remarkable cures. We have not as yet attached sufficient importance to sunlight as a regular remedy for good health, and I doubt whether it would be possible to over- stress the health-value of sunlight as food.

Page 392

CHAPTER II. 

You cannot assure yourself of having or keeping good health by living for pleasure and personal appearance. You cannot be well and strong, or for long beautiful and winsome, or handsome and magnetic, through the single or combined efforts of the style designers, clothing manufacturers and merchants, cosmetic makers, and theatre and dance hall managers, or on a bath, soft garments, six hours of work, six hours of eating eye-charming food with ear-tickling gossip as a general flavor, six hours of entertainment, two hours of riding, walking and time-wasting, and four hours of sleep. Herein is neither a day's work, nor a day's happiness, nor a night's rest, but strictly a frenzied dissipation of time, health, efficiency and personal vivacity and charm. This manner of living is one of a sort which I decry, and for the alteration of which I have studied out this health plan. 

Afraid to Be Frank With Yourself

After thinking over your case, I have concluded that it might be advisable for you to ask your physician to set aside an hour for a conference. However, if you prefer not to see him, but save the fee (for a foolish novelty which you have seen in a store window), I offer you the following substitute for a talk. Imagine that you are in consultation with the physician and are determined to describe candidly your manner of living. 

You review your day to him; you tell him what you do, what you eat, how you spend your time. You are very frank, in order that he may have facts on which to base his advice. 

Candid? You are afraid to be candid with yourself.

Page 393

You make one excuse after another for this, that and something else. It is about all that you can do to square yourself with yourself. 

Your physician, who is a scientific expert and shrewd judge of human nature, can diagnose your case-for you are the average-by looking at you. Being familiar with symptoms, he is able to read your dissipations from your eyes, lips, countenance and bearing. Frenzied living! He knows.

If you partially offset the effects of dissipation with three to four hours' sleep, and delay the physical collapse or shattering of your nerves for a year or two, what could you not do for your health of mind and body by adopting the principles which I herein recommend? You are not well. Living as recklessly as you do, you could not be. You cannot waste your health, yet preserve it.

Self-Deception By Superstition 

Most men and women dread the idea of paying special attention to their health, as, for example, going to a physician for a special examination, or staying home for a day or two on account of indisposition, or taking a rest. They feel that the very idea is an admission of the approach of illness, or that it is weakening of will which would bring on sickness. They have a superstition about a health vacation. To take a rest, they feel, is to give up. They have a presentiment, rather, an emotion somewhat like a presentiment, that a rest would be a surrender.

The feeling is not unlike that of the man who thinks in latter years of retiring from business. Although he Page 394

desires to do so, and thinks that enjoyment of leisure would be good for him, he is reluctant to give up responsibility and work. As he considers retiring he already senses a void, lack of interest and idleness.

Others feel that there is too much or unnecessary trouble in connection with a health-vacation. They will have to do this and that, and avoid this and that, and eat this and that, and refrain from this and that. 

All their minutes, thoughts and acts will be regulated arbitrarily by prescription, they muse, and they are loathe to be ruled by strict discipline. 

Well, in the first place, taking a health-vacation is not giving up, retiring, or conceding breakdown of health.

On the contrary, it is the only means of averting either of these misfortunes. There is not one person in 

100,000 who is not so debilitated in health in one way or another by the rigors of business or society in our complex civilization, or the worries inseparable from prevailing conditions, or the general strain of work and responsibilities of different sorts, or reckless or careless living, as to require a brief vacation. The mode of living and working is such that the vacation of at least a day every week is necessary.

You Develop Your Own Easy Plan 

Secondly, there is not, as I have illustrated to you, any strict discipline or hard sacrifice connected with the trial of the plan which I suggest in a brief healthvacation. I do not ask you to be governed by strict regulations, but to take the week or few weeks easy and rebuild your health. I merely recommend that you adopt the fundamentals of my plan and build up a sys- Page 395

tem of your own, suited to your circumstances, your likes and your state of health. On the vacation you would, if your health is more or less deteriorated, follow the directions rather closely, for your object would be to recuperate; if you are in good health, and need only a rest and toning-up, you would be more lenient in following the suggestions.

Everybody who feels that he has slipped some or is beginning to slip is timid about treatment and medicine. 

He has either conscious or subconscious fear. This symptom is characteristic of most of the modern diseases, in which the nervous system generally is directly or secondarily affected. To some extent, it is a wholesome state of mind, as it indicates serious regard for health. In our fast living, which allows no time for thought of health, it is an encouraging sign. 

When one nowadays senses nervous or other physical weakness or intensity, it is the moment to act quickly to prevent decline. Nobody can afford to ignore minor symptoms under existing circumstances. We are so indifferent concerning health that most of us are near a breakdown before we realize it. 

Aid to Physician's Bank Account

It is better, however, not to wait for the greater or minor symptoms. Each of us has many urgent reasons to believe that his health is impaired, though, perhaps, imperceptibly as yet, by the manner of living. Existing conditions and prevailing habits are conducive to health impairment, and, as I have said, the average person is not in prime health. We cannot live as we do and expect to have good health. Each must concede this fact to himself.

Page 396

I most seriously suggest a physical examination by the family physician. Such an examination should be had at least once a year in these days of fast working, furious living, intense worry and inattention to health. Strong and adaptive as the human organism is, it cannot stand up against suicidal methods.

The manner of living unquestionably is suicidal. I do not declare that it is deliberately so. But the circumstance of ignorance or carelessness does not dispose of the fact. We are living at a self-killing speed and pressure.

I maintain not only that everybody should have the physical examination once or twice a year in order to find out what impairment is going on in the body or mind, and to find out the cause, but also that he should take a vacation for recuperation once a year and also that he should take a day off every week or two and get out of his environment. These precautions are practically necessary for maintaining normal health. 

You should correct the erroneous idea that the periodical vacation and recuperative course would be irksome. You have it because you are fixed mentally and physically in habit; and this habit is one of the principal reasons why the change which the vacation would afford you is absolutely necessary. You are living in grooves of habit. You think in ruts. You are dominated completely by this habit, and it would be impossible for you to alter your views or make a change in your manner of living without getting away from the environment in which the habit is based. Unless you get away from business, home and friends, from the situation in which your habit of thought and employ- Page 397

ment is effective with you, there is very little chance, if any, that you would obtain a modified perspective as to yourself and your affairs. 

Retard! Stop!! Reverse!!! 

If you feel rundown, or sense impending debilitation, you should call on your physician immediately. He will find out the cause of the decline. If you feel that your health is still fairly good, and no fear or apprehension bothers you, it is not necessary to have the examination before arranging for the vacation. At any rate, you need the vacation, as it will recuperate your body and brighten your intellect and give you a broader outlook on your own life and problems. new and

Decide that you will take up the natural plan of living which I recommend. I advise you against taking up the schedule in its entirety at the beginning. I prefer that you at first grasp thoroughly the principles which I expound, that is, that you should come to accord with the philosophy and conclude that in order to preserve your health at constant prime it is necessary to abide by the cosmic laws. 

Do not tire yourself on the vacation, or become disgusted at the suggestions. Your vacation and self-service should be so agreeable and efficacious as to convince you of the logic and merit of my plan. 

Retard! Stop! Reverse!

CHAPTER III. 

Forget About Calendar and Clock

Now, there are three regular stages in the ageing process in which time is an important element. Grow- Page 398

ing old, however, is a decline or decay which is not exclusively a result of time; the process is instigated and hastened by other causes than the increase of years. 

Reckless or careless living, which brings about premature physical corruption or organic disease, is, with many persons, of greater deadening consequence than time.

The human life-span I divide into three stages-21 years each—coincident in the practical way with the mutations emanating from the combination of time and experience. I disregard the period from birth up to the twenty-eighth year, as it is the most constructive or formative. From the health standpoint, in relation to time and experience in human destiny, the three divisions are: First, Middle-Aged People, 28 to 49 years; Elderly People, 49 to 70 years, and Old People, 70 years and more.

The stage of the Middle Age is the most active. It is the stage in which ambition is quickest and work hardest and most enterprising, the stage in which the individual applies his talents and develops his designs toward achievement, wealth, success and possible happiness. He lives at the maximum of energy during this stage, and to a great extent he forms all his habits and opinions, and stabilizes or abandons his ideals and principles. It may be judged, from every angle, as being the most vital stage, the most vital physically, intellectually, morally, economically. It brings out the best and worst in him. 

Age of Culture and Philosophy

The stage of the Elderly Age is that of intellectual and technical or occupational prime. The prime of ac- Page 399

tivity is said to be between thirty-five and forty-five, but we may rightly say that the cumulative prime, when the philosophy of experience equalizes with the flight of time and the utilization of energy, begins, or should begin, about forty-five. 

No one should be old at forty-five or forty-nine; he should then be most efficient. While he would not be so active physically, the power and reserve derived from his philosophy, technique, skill and experience would more than compensate for possible or probable slowing-up of physical effort. He would be able to conserve energy and at the same time be more efficient and productive. In the normal course of human events it is generally believed, however, that the individual's epoch of leisure nears on and after forty-nine, and from fiftyfive or so into the future he takes more ease and considers retirement.

The consequences on mind, health and spirit of the stage of activity are felt by every one in this second stage, and in our mad manner of living the crisis as to health is apparent at any moment between twentyeight and forty-nine, largely independent of the effects of time. 

The third stage, that of Old Age, 70 years and beyond! What is it? A period of mental clarity and physical soundness, of comfort, meditation and happiness? Or a period of intellectual dullness, physical decay and remorse? It should be the former, and can be.

I divide the span of human life into the three stages which conform to the mutations and destiny of man, that is, the three normal stages in order Page 400

to illustrate more lucidly the consistency of my plan for constant prime of health to the natural progression. 

Nature marks out these three stages definitely. 

Retard! Stop! Reverse! I say. In the first stage, (old age) retard! in the second (elderly) stop! in the third (middle age) reverse! the ageing process. Or in any stage, especially in the first or second-Retard! 

Stop! Reverse! As I have explained clearly, time is but one factor in the ageing process. In some casesperhaps, nowadays, in many-time is a minor element in ageing. 

Tossing Human Machine on Scrap Pile

Business concerns are scrapping men of thirty-five to forty-five at the present time. Why? Because men are old at thirty-five to forty-five, or begin to slip, or diminish in efficiency? Whatever the reasons, there is only one inference, which is that business believes that men begin to show incompetency on or after thirty-five, or, in plain terms, they are intellectually or physically aged. 

Business decides that senility begins sometime between thirty-five and forty-five.

Do not think, please, that I fully agree with this viewpoint of business. There is, I think, imbecility in business that depreciates the value and efficiency of men of experience in the prime of life. Business itself cannot be competent or efficient when it discards brains for action. In the economic depression business would have been better off had it retained brains and experience and attached less importance to deceptive statistics. Business, as we know, aims to standardize the human element to the fullest extent, and it would do Page 401

this by employing the very young, who are impressionable, and training them to be machines. Then, it would require a very few superintendents, having intelligence, who would also function as machines. Business considers that men of thirty-five to forty-five have formed opinions from their experience and habits from their routine and are not tractable enough, because they are thinkers. Standardized and mechanized and routine business has a few serious lessons to learn, one of which is that intelligence and experience are as essential to economy and efficiency of production as is action. Business is experimenting with extravagant economy. 

Why Not Scrap Some Executives?

The age-limit rule which business has lately enforced implies that executives in some lines of business are willing to wear out the human units at thirty-five to fortyfive. It is a unique, unreasonable and inhumane theory. 

Nevertheless, it will be enforced until its fallacy is demonstrated.

The new dictum of business, misguided and unscientific though it is, is another practical argument to the individual that he must take care of himself. He cannot work in a self-devastating frenzy and live in a selfdestructive frenzy and retain his health. To consume health in work and ruin it in pleasure leads to one inevitable consequence-an early breakdown and premature death.

A new order is possible. It is possible to retard the ageing process. It is possible to stop decline. It is possible even to reverse the ageing process, especially in youth or middle age. The man who has good health and Page 402

preserves it, or who in time recovers it and thereafter exercises ordinary care, can retard, then stop and then reverse the suicidal process. 

You agree, doubtless, that the ageing process can be retarded, or possibly stopped and postponed, but you may doubt the prospect of reversing it. The last, you may reason, is too much to expect. However, after giving the subject more thought, I feel sure that you will agree also in this ultimate conclusion. 

CHAPTER IV. 

Means of Retarding Ageing Process 

How can you retard the ageing process?

No matter how old the individual is, twenty-eight, forty-nine, seventy, he can retard ageing. It is enough to abandon the self-destructive practices and substitute measures conforming to the requirements of nature. 

To do this is more than prudent; it is absolutely necessary.

The person who is working furiously and living fast is outracing time. He is committing suicide and cutting ten, twenty or more years off his life-span; or, which is worse, he is speeding on to an early total and final physical collapse, which will land him permanently in an insane asylum or hospital for incurables, there to spend the years which should be most useful and agreeable in despondency and misery. He may take his choice now. He may retard the ageing process, or take the consequences described or something similar. 

The furious and fast liver eliminates time from his life. This is very plain. This being the case, it is equally Page 403

plain that the factor of time can be counteracted or compensated for by living according to the principles of nature. The contrast explains from life's experience the actual significance of my contention that ageing can be retarded and stopped, and even, by timely recuperation, reversed.

Tick-Tock! Ding-Dong! Tube Roses 

Time, an element of nature, is inexorable. Time wanes with the decline of health. There is a precise moment in the life of the furious and fast liver at which the tocsin is rung. It is the precise moment at which time and self-destruction converge and determine by natural law that the last chance to save health is extinct.

Therefore, the gravamen of my counsel is that the individual who desires health, happiness and long life must procure them while he can. 

While you can, preserve or save your health! The ageing process can be reversed by the average person at a precise moment in the stage of 28 to 49. I need not caution you that the ability diminishes year after year, in some instances month after month, to reverse the ageing process. The person who delays attending to his health until he is 50 cannot hope to achieve more than to stop the ageing process and leave his fate with time; after fifty he can do no more, or scarcely more, than retain such health as he may have. On and after 70, if he has been careless of health, probably the best that he can do is to retard the ageing process. 

While you can, preserve or save your health! Later on it will be too late. The opportune time for rejuvenation is in the first stage-Middle Age. The human or- Page 404

ganism has a strange and wonderful power of adaptation toward remaking itself, as has been indicated by an occasional improvement and spontaneous disappearance of malignant diseases. This power is equivalent to the combination of time and the state of health; that is, it is vigorous almost throughout the first stage of the human life-span, for in this period the body has recuperative energy ample to overcome and reverse deterioration. When, as a consequence of neglect of health and the lapse of time, this recuperative energy is moribund, the power of adaptation is inert.

While Recuperative Power Is Quick 

I tell you, not that you can reverse nature, but that you must comply with nature's requirements. I tell you that you must begin to respect nature while you can, and, if you do not, nature will have no respect for you. 

You do not reverse nature when you reverse the ageing process, but conform to nature. 

Why do I emphasize recuperation and adoption of a natural manner of living in the first stage-Middle 

Age? Principally because it is not too late to change habits completely, and because, too, the body has not only the recuperative power, but also the recuperative impulse or energy. There is more physical power of will in this stage; there is more reserve of physical power, there is more energy for comeback. Furthermore, self-destruction has not yet gone to the immedicable extreme.

While I say that the prospects of recuperation are darker in the latter ages, the Elderly and Old, I do not concede that they have perished. As long as there is life, Page 405

there is some chance of recuperation of health, provided, of course, the body is not a wreck. I stress the obvious fact that the expectation of health-improvement is brighter in Middle Age than in either of the two latter, and in Middle Age time and physical condition are more favorable, yet I do not admit that it is hopeless in the latter ages. Logically, any serious effort toward fundamental betterment of health, at any time, naturally must be effective. 

Better Sooner Than Later 

Destruction of health can be reversed, and should be.

Any time is appropriate. The natural principle for reversing the digressive trend is to adopt living practices conforming to the cosmic laws, and to do this is easy, rather than difficult. The only hard obstacle is the habits of dissipation which have been formed; for habits, being second nature, are seldom given up or modified easily. I have explained that dissipation is not only waste and extravagance of physical and nervous energy in the pursuit of pleasure, but waste and extravagance in work, diversion and general activities. 

It must be clear to you, without further exemplification, that destruction of health can be reversed at any time. Also, it must be clear to you that the reversal can be accomplished chiefly by individual hygiene. The individual must do it himself. The physician cannot change the habits which ruin health; this must be done by the individual. The causes of physical destruction, tending inevitably toward physical or mental collapse, lie in the individual's manner of living. They cannot be reached by science, but exclusively by the individual. Page 406

The causes must be removed by shifting from a destructive to a protective manner of living. 

The sooner the natural method of living is substituted for the suicidal the better the assurance must be for continuous prime of health, with the happiness and peace of mind concomitant with it, and for a long span of life. As long as there is a reserve of vigor and buoyancy of energy, the opportunity exists for reversing the ageing process and adding years to the life-span; but when either of these is depreciated, we must think, not of reversing the ageing process, but of stopping or retarding it. Stopping the ageing process in the Elderly Age or retarding it in the Old Age is effective in improving health and expanding age. But the best precaution and measure are, of course, to reverse the ageing process in the opportunity of time and health-reserve by taking up the natural plan of living.

Your Own Plan to This Standard 

The plan which I recommend to you for alternating rest and exercise naturally, and using the proper nutrition in quantity, variety and quality, coincides with the cosmic principles. It guarantees, through the vacation and training, followed by observance of the ordinary suggestions, maintained prime of health and prolongation of the span of life. Besides, it guarantees the happiness which is associated with harmony and tranquillity. 

There is nothing hard or irksome about the plan. You accept it is as the standard, but modify it to suit your circumstances. That is, from the schedule which I submit, you build up a plan of your own better than anybody else can. Take a short vacation and live as closely Page 407

to the standard as you feel that you can, and after the vacation develop your own permanent plan. 

You will find out from experience that your health will improve and will be sustained at constant prime, and you will be more efficient and spirited. To your surprise you will find out from experience that it is easier to live naturally than madly, and that happiness is more gratifying than pleasure.

THE IMPORT. 

As we end our excursion in nature's wonderland and our rambles into domains of science, we tarry for a few moments to consider the entrancing views and interesting concepts and opinions which were unfolded before us. What, we should ask ourselves, are we living for?

So-called success and pleasure by fast and furious activity? Or health and happiness by a natural manner of living, with prolonged existence and tranquil years of leisure? 

Each of us may make his choice. He may cram frenzy of work and folly into a few years, and die prematurely, or break down in health when he should be in his prime. Or, he may do his work more efficiently and toward surer and greater success, and enjoy true happiness and content in a long life of sound health. 

Distinguished physicians warn us that the present pace in work and pleasure, the whole reckless manner of living, is suicidal. They warn us that we can expect no further relief from science. Our only assurance lies in Individual Hygiene and in each of us practicing preventive medicine himself. The conclusions of these noted physicians and scientists are final.

Page 408

So, the question is up to you!

I have given you proof from nature that it is only necessary for you to live by rules of common sense in compliance with natural principles in order to prolong your life expectancy and have continual health and happiness in constant prime. You do not have to change your mode of living radically; it is enough to follow nature in a plan of your own arrangement from the standard which I have described. 

Retard! Stop! Reverse!

It has been demonstrated to you scientifically that the ageing process can be reversed at any moment in the three sections of the span of life by conforming to nature's requirements and following the cosmic principles. As long as one opposes the empirical laws of nature, he cannot expect to be healthy, or remain healthy, as his living method is self-destructive. 

The first and best opportunity for reversing the ageing process is in the stage of Middle Age, covering the years between 28 and 49; the chance is not quite so propitious in the second stage, Elderly People, 49 to 70 years, but it still exists, and prompt, drastic action is certain to be effective; while in the third stage, Old 

People, 70 years and more, the lingering promise is to retard the ageing process and add a few years to life. 

Turn back to the schedule which I submitted, outlining the standard for natural living, and seriously work out a plan, suited to your particular circumstances, for common sense living and the resultant satisfaction of constant prime of health, happiness and a longer life expectancy.

Page 409

Solemnly, I leave the problem with you. It is yours.

The almost inevitable outlook of the suicidal manner of living, which distinguished physicians condemn, is either early death or mental, nervous or general physical breakdown. This is the bleak prospect ahead of each of us who is antagonizing nature. You sensed this dismal probability even before the physicians promulgated their warning.

Beneficent nature offers to you bounteous gifts for a healthy and happy existence and a longer span of years. The only condition prescribed is that you shall use these gifts in a spirit of appreciation by adhering to the cosmic principles. Nature, too, leaves the decision with you.
